About AI discoveries in 2026

This selection contains 1840 catalog entries dated 1 Jan 2026 to 25 Nov 2026. 1839 include structured evidence annotations describing reported tests, the AI contribution and remaining limits. Missing annotations mean those details have not been structured here, not that a study lacks evidence.

Dates follow the catalog publication policy. Coverage is selected and incomplete; entries can overlap or describe several results. Counts do not measure independent discoveries, clinical benefit or worldwide research output. Read the selection and evidence policy or compare catalog coverage by year and field.

Discoveries

25 Nov 2026Johns Hopkins University; Johns Hopkins School of Medicine

Deep learning predicts DNA damage response protein levels from routine slides

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Gated attention-based multiple-instance learning on frozen pathology foundation-model tile embeddings predicted reverse-phase protein array pathway scores from 1,673 TCGA haematoxylin-and-eosin whole-slide images covering 1,527 patients with breast, endometrial, cervical and ovarian cancer and uterine carcinosarcoma. For the 11-protein DNA damage composite the out-of-fold Spearman correlation was 0.43 against 0.31 for a cancer-type-mean baseline, about 0.29-0.30 within cancer type, and at most 0.00 with shuffled targets. Cross-validation was cancer-type-stratified and site-grouped. This is a moderate retrospective association in one public cohort: there is no external validation, no clinical or treatment-response outcome, and the authors list separating the signal from proliferation as future work, noting the cell-cycle score was predicted more strongly.

Technology: Gated attention-based multiple-instance learning over frozen UNI2-h and Virchow2 tile embeddings jointly regresses six RPPA pathway scores.

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The attention-based model predicts DNA damage response protein scores from slide-tile embeddings.
Test and result
Five-fold cancer-type-stratified, site-grouped cross-validation against measured RPPA values, compared with a cancer-type-mean baseline and a shuffled-target control.
Limitation / still unknown
No external cohort; no clinical or therapy-response outcome; the signal is not separated from proliferation; within-type correlation is moderate; not peer reviewed.

25 Nov 2026European Commission, Joint Research Centre; University of Valencia; Integrated Food Security Phase Classification Global Support Unit

Machine learning forecasts acute food crises and separates predictive from causal drivers

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Missing-data-resilient machine-learning pipelines, including gradient boosting and a tabular foundation model, forecast acute food crisis occurrence 3, 6 and 9 months ahead across 80 countries using monthly subnational data for 2007-2024, exceeding no-skill baselines by 15 to 50 percentage points in PR-AUC under nested cross-validation. TreeSHAP profiles fell into three recurring archetypes. Doubly robust causal estimates indicated that acute conflict events, which carried the weakest predictive signal, are primary causal drivers, while agroclimatology was the only dimension where predictive and causal importance agreed. The authors report that skill tracks crisis prevalence, that the causal effects are modest and only moderately robust to unmeasured confounding, and that they depend on an assumed causal graph; several variables were reported as non-identifiable. The work is a preprint.

Technology: Tree-based and tabular foundation-model classifiers with TreeSHAP attribution and cross-fitted doubly robust causal estimation.

Editorial relevance 40 multidisciplinary Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The learned forecasters produce the crisis predictions whose attributions are compared with the causal estimates.
Test and result
Retrospective nested cross-validation on harmonized food-insecurity data with FEWS NET and IPC/CH labels, plus a held-out explainability period.
Limitation / still unknown
Skill tracks crisis prevalence; causal estimates are modest, depend on an assumed causal graph and are only moderately robust to unmeasured confounding; no prospective operational test; not peer reviewed.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

25 Nov 2026University of Washington; Pacific Northwest National Laboratory

LLM-agent workflow reports new proofs on positivity problems in algebraic combinatorics

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint describes PatternMiner, in which an LLM agent generates mathematical datasets, trains small models (MLPs, small transformers, trees), interprets them and formulates conjectures that are tested against counterexamples in mathematical software, applied to five positivity problems from Stanley's survey. The paper gives proofs, labelled LLM-generated, that for every n>=5 no fixed number of retained flag dimensions suffices for projection tests of Schubert structure-constant vanishing, an equivariant positivity inequality that proves Conjectures A and B of Chen et al. on equivariant nonvanishing, a character-row evaluation formula, a restricted case of Kazhdan-Lusztig combinatorial invariance, and Schur positivity for a family of immanant coefficients, supported by finite checks such as 776,891 coefficients over 1,587 shape instances. Proofs received an internal adversarial review only; no proof-kernel or independent mathematician check is reported, one decisive construction needed human redirection, and the comparison with direct LLM attempts is not compute-matched.

Technology: Unnamed LLM agent that trains and interprets small models (activation patching, probes, sparse autoencoders), proposes conjectures and attempts proofs, with counterexample searches in mathematical software.

Editorial relevance 60 mathematics PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
An LLM agent designs experiments, trains narrow models on mathematical data, interprets them and proposes and attempts proofs of conjectures.
Test and result
Proofs given in the paper with an internal adversarial review, plus finite computational checks of the stated identities and positivity claims, ablations of model interpretation and comparison with direct LLM attempts.
Limitation / still unknown
No Lean or other proof-kernel verification and no independent mathematician review reported; human redirection at one decisive point; the LLM powering the agent is not named; limited to algebraic combinatorics; baselines not compute-matched; not peer reviewed.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33966 · https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33966

24 Nov 2026Xinjiang Institute of Technology; Lanzhou University; Lanzhou Jiaotong University

Interpretable machine learning maps wastewater treatment conditions

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An XGBoost model fitted to 1,423 experimental and literature-derived samples linked iron/carbon ratio and operating conditions to chemical oxygen demand removal in polyvinyl-alcohol wastewater treatment. SHAP analysis identified iron/carbon ratio as the strongest modeled factor and a pH-dependent interaction window. Reported test R² was 0.8045, but the paper selected its final model using that test set, limiting an unbiased generalization claim. The preprint offers model-derived process associations, not a confirmed causal mechanism or validated industrial-scale control policy.

Technology: XGBoost learns COD-removal response from experimental and literature-derived process data; SHAP attributes nonlinear, condition-dependent associations to operating variables.

Editorial relevance 40 chemistry PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
XGBoost learns COD-removal response from experimental and literature-derived process data; SHAP attributes nonlinear, condition-dependent associations to operating variables.
Test and result
Seven-model comparison on a mixed 1,423-sample dataset; interpretation of Fe/C ratio, pH and other process conditions.
Limitation / still unknown
Model selection used the held-out test set; literature/source dependence, causal interpretation, real-effluent transfer and industrial-scale outcomes are unresolved.

24 Nov 2026University Medical Center Utrecht; Meta VCI Map consortium; Heinrich Heine University Düsseldorf; Research Centre Jülich

Learned lesion-network models identify shared and specific stroke cognition patterns

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 preprint analyzed 3,034 patients across 13 stroke cohorts using learned models of lesion anatomy, network connectivity and clinical characteristics. Structural lesion connectivity supplied most of the gain beyond clinical information and infarct size, with left anterior thalamic connectivity consistently informative across cognitive outcomes. Benefits persisted for several outcomes when entire cohorts were held out. The results support a bounded lesion-in-context account of cognitive impairment, without establishing causal circuitry or clinical readiness.

Technology: Stacked ridge classifiers and regularized logistic meta-learner; interpretable feature ablation and cross-cohort prediction

Editorial relevance 53 medicine PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Ridge base classifiers learned complementary lesion representations and a logistic stacking model combined out-of-fold predictions. Ablation, stable coefficients and unseen-cohort tests linked predictive gains to specific network anatomy rather than lesion volume alone.
Test and result
Nested repeated cross-validation evaluated 3,034 patients from 13 cohorts. Adding anatomy and connectivity improved six of seven targets; any-domain impairment AUPRC rose from 0.67 to 0.71. Leave-one-cohort-out tests retained significant gains for three of seven targets by normalized AUPRC. Stable model coefficients highlighted shared thalamic connectivity and domain-specific anatomical profiles.
Limitation / still unknown
Retrospective pooled cohorts were heterogeneous and predominantly represented mild strokes and Asian or European ancestry. Cognitive outcomes were binary at one assessment within a year, with premorbid cognition unavailable. Cross-cohort performance varied and several domain gains did not transfer significantly. Prediction and coefficient stability do not prove causation or clinical utility.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.11.10.765579v1.full.pdf

No follow-up recorded; this is not a claim that no follow-up exists. Follow-up review scope and date are not recorded. No source-supported relationships recorded.

24 Nov 2026Shahid Bahonar University of Kerman; University of Saskatchewan; Sport Science Research Institute

Machine learning links multidomain profiles to fall history

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In a cross-sectional study of 147 community-dwelling adults aged 55 and older, machine-learning models classified ordered past-year fall-history groups from physical-function, activity, health and social measures. Random Forest reported four-class accuracy of 0.946 and binary AUC of 0.968 under internal five-fold validation after excluding fall-location, fall-situation and fear-of-falling variables. The preprint describes associations with recorded fall history, not prediction of future falls. A small, imbalanced single-region sample and self-reported measures limit generalization; external and prospective validation remain necessary.

Technology: Random Forest, logistic regression, gradient boosting and SVM learn multidomain associations with past-year fall-history strata; feature importance characterizes the internally evaluated profile.

Editorial relevance 40 medicine PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Random Forest, logistic regression, gradient boosting and SVM learn multidomain associations with past-year fall-history strata; feature importance characterizes the internally evaluated profile.
Test and result
Stratified five-fold internal classification of fall-history strata in 147 adults, excluding specified post-fall variables.
Limitation / still unknown
Cross-sectional history, self-report and small uneven strata; no external validation, causal effects or demonstrated future-fall prediction.

24 Nov 2026OpenAI; Onava

GPT-6.2 protein designs show binding across five targets in laboratory tests

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: OpenAI reports that Onava laboratory tests found binding for 35 of 322 minibinders based on GPT-6.2-designed structures, with at least one hit for each of five targets. GPT-6.2 generated backbone designs using reasoning and general-purpose code, while SolubleMPNN supplied sequences and specialized models helped select candidates. The November 24, 2026 announcement links a preprint; the findings do not establish an end-to-end workflow without specialized models, independent replication, specificity, therapeutic activity or clinical benefit.

Technology: GPT-6.2; SolubleMPNN; specialized protein-model candidate filtering

Family GPT-6: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 biology Blog postPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A prerelease GPT-6.2 generated proposed three-dimensional minibinder backbones from target structures through reasoning and general-purpose code; SolubleMPNN supplied sequences and specialized structure-prediction models filtered candidates before laboratory tests.
Test and result
The first-party announcement reports 35 binding-positive proteins among 322 laboratory-tested minibinders across five targets, based on GPT-6.2 backbone designs followed by specialized sequence generation and filtering. Onava was the study laboratory partner, not independent follow-up.
Limitation / still unknown
The linked preprint Methods were not read in this review; detailed assay conditions and binding thresholds were not assessed. Binding does not establish specificity, useful biological activity, safety or therapeutic efficacy. End-to-end design without specialized-model assistance and independent replication are not established.

24 Nov 2026Sorbonne Université; CNRS; ESPCI Paris; Institut Polytechnique de Paris; Sapienza University of Rome

Generative models guide ribozymes that retain activity at high temperature

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 preprint reports laboratory-tested catalytic RNA designs that remain active at 60°C. A learned evolutionary sequence model combined with an independent RNA thermodynamic score generated variants that preserved function more often than either score alone. Across 2,500 designed variants from ten starting sequences, the jointly favored group had a 72.5% high-temperature activity hit rate, compared with 7.8% for the evolutionary-score group and 0.4% for the physical-score group. The result is limited to one ribozyme family and the study’s assay.

Technology: Edge-activation direct coupling analysis (DCA); learned Potts sequence model combined with RNA thermodynamics

Editorial relevance 60 biology PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
An edge-activation DCA model learned family-specific sequence constraints from an alignment of 817 natural sequences. Combining its generative prior with a separately derived thermodynamic score guided designs whose catalytic activity was then measured.
Test and result
Laboratory activity measurements compared 1,500 jointly favored designs with 500 evolutionary-score and 500 physical-score designs, pooled across ten starting sequences. High-temperature activity hit rates were 72.5%, 7.8% and 0.4%, respectively, under the study’s reference-normalized threshold.
Limitation / still unknown
The study examines one self-splicing RNA family and uses a pooled enrichment assay rather than general application performance. Molecular dynamics provides mechanistic interpretation, not independent experimental confirmation of the proposed structural explanation. Independent replication and transfer to other families remain unestablished.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.11.21.768297v1.full.pdf

24 Nov 2026McGill University; Université de Montréal; Mila

AI-designed visual stimuli probe how perceptual learning changes decisions

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 preprint used the learned DorsalNet visual model to design motion stimuli targeting different model stages, then tested training in 19 human observers. After three days, ten observers trained on late-stage stimuli showed increased perceptual sensitivity, while nine trained on early-stage stimuli mainly changed response bias. Transfer tests and model comparisons were consistent with a late-stage readout account. Cortical stages were not directly recorded, and separate participant groups limit causal comparison.

Technology: DorsalNet; learned neural visual model and gradient-based stimulus synthesis

Editorial relevance 53 biology PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
DorsalNet, trained on self-motion from naturalistic videos, enabled stimulus synthesis selectively separating early or late model-layer responses. Human tests supplied new behavioral evidence relevant to competing perceptual-learning accounts.
Test and result
Nineteen observers trained for three days. Late-stage-group sensitivity increased (n=10; paired p=0.001); early-stage-group change was not significant (n=9; p=0.100), while its response criterion changed. Transfer across location and stimulus type informed comparisons with alternative model readouts.
Limitation / still unknown
A difference in within-group significance does not itself prove a between-group learning interaction. Separate groups may differ before training; stimulus-to-cortical-stage correspondence was inferred from the model rather than directly measured. Model–human specificity agreement was assessed qualitatively, and energetic interpretations remain speculative.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.11.23.768712v1.full.pdf

24 Nov 2026Tally Health; University of California, San Diego; TruDiagnostic

Epigenetic deep learning detects a lifestyle-trial signature

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: CheekAI neural networks trained on 25,400 buccal methylation profiles from 14,716 adults were applied retrospectively to external datasets. In an existing eight-week randomized lifestyle trial with 18 intervention and 20 control participants, the model’s age-adjusted score decreased by 1.34 model-years relative to controls (p = 0.021), largely through dietary and metabolic proxy components. This preprint reports a learned methylation-score response, not demonstrated reversal of biological aging or improved health. The score was designed to reflect lifestyle proxies; a separate semaglutide analysis did not significantly reduce the overall score and showed tissue-transfer limitations.

Technology: Neural networks predict age and epigenetic lifestyle/health biomarker proxies from methylation principal components; a learned additive combination enables interpretable retrospective response analysis.

Editorial relevance 47 biology PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Neural networks predict age and epigenetic lifestyle/health biomarker proxies from methylation principal components; a learned additive combination enables interpretable retrospective response analysis.
Test and result
Person-grouped nested cross-validation, replicate testing and retrospective analysis of existing intervention/control and external methylation datasets.
Limitation / still unknown
Lifestyle-weighted score is not a validated biological-aging or health-benefit endpoint; small external trial, tissue transfer and proxy interpretation limit inference.

24 Nov 2026Laboratoire de Physique de l’École Normale Supérieure, ENS, Université PSL, CNRS, Sorbonne Université, Université Paris Cité; Laboratoire Jean Perrin, Sorbonne Université, CNRS

Learned cell-shape signatures predict confined transit

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Support-vector and logistic classifiers linked cell area and circularity to whether MDA-MB-231 breast-cancer cells would traverse microfluidic constrictions. Less circular cells were more likely to transit, and trajectory features about 30 minutes before an event were most informative. Models transferred without retraining to narrower constrictions and more adhesive surfaces. This preprint reports a condition-dependent association in one cell line, not a causal mechanism or a predictor of metastasis in patients.

Technology: Support-vector classification with an RBF kernel and logistic regression learn transit signatures from cell/nuclear contour trajectories; feature selection and Shapley analysis identify predictive shape descriptors.

Editorial relevance 53 biology PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Support-vector classification with an RBF kernel and logistic regression learn transit signatures from cell/nuclear contour trajectories; feature selection and Shapley analysis identify predictive shape descriptors.
Test and result
Trajectory-level train/validation/test partitions, comparison with random-classifier baselines and transfer to narrower constrictions and adhesive surfaces.
Limitation / still unknown
One cell line; observational predictive associations, no causal perturbation establishing morphology as mechanism and no clinical metastasis validation.
Evidence sources and annotation context

24 Nov 2026Sun Yat-sen University; Guangdong Pharmaceutical University; East China Normal University

Learned brain-activity profiles guide tested drug leads in zebrafish models

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 preprint combined large-scale zebrafish brain imaging, learned clustering and random-forest ranking to select drug conditions for follow-up. Several candidates improved movement in larval toxin models and preserved dopamine-neuron reporter signals under some conditions. These are preliminary, compound- and model-dependent findings rather than evidence of Parkinson’s disease treatment in people. Some screened conditions were toxic, and retained fluorescence does not by itself establish prevention of neuronal death.

Technology: CNSDIP; consensus clustering and random-forest ranking of brain-activity maps

Editorial relevance 47 biology PreprintScientific article Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Consensus clustering linked drug-induced brain-activity patterns to a therapeutic reference class; a random forest trained on 363 CNS/non-CNS drugs then ranked candidates for new behavioral and cellular tests. The measured follow-up, rather than classifier accuracy alone, is the admitted result.
Test and result
Sixteen top-ranked conditions underwent follow-up; after toxicity exclusions, five compounds and one pair improved movement in tested conditions. Behavioral comparisons used n=10 and dopamine-reporter cell-count comparisons n=5. Blinded imaging counts supported preservation of identifiable reporter-positive cells for some candidates across three larval toxin models.
Limitation / still unknown
The assays are small and preclinical; not every candidate worked across every model or outcome. Five conditions showed long-term toxicity. Reporter loss or preservation can reflect neuronal phenotype rather than irreversible death or true neuroprotection. Known drug mechanisms explain some effects, and neither efficacy nor safety in people is established.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11797074/v1.pdf

Source checked · editorial details

The source page was checked against this entry. Independent confirmation is not assessed by this label.

Review date: 25 Nov 2026. Review scope: Primary 37-page preprint PDF relevant CNSDIP model description, experimental Results, blinded counting Methods and Figure 6–7 sample counts; primary version metadata. Earlier cited platform abstracts reviewed for result identity; supplements not audited.

24 Nov 2026Columbia University Irving Medical Center

Bivexia selects an interaction-selective LAG-3 inhibitor tested in mouse models

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 bioRxiv preprint reports that Bivexia used random-forest predictions of four protein-interaction assays to select BVX-01, a small molecule that disrupted LAG-3 interactions with tau and amyloid-beta while preserving two other tested interactions. The authors report nanomolar LAG-3 binding, activity in donor-derived neurons, and reduced pathology readouts in two mouse models, with two cohorts per model. This is preclinical evidence from the discovery study, not peer-reviewed or independent confirmation or evidence of clinical benefit; the full cascade rests on one compound, and broader target campaigns stopped at primary screening.

24 Nov 2026Jinggangshan University; The Chinese University of Hong Kong; Shenzhen University of Advanced Technology; Shenzhen People’s Hospital

Unsupervised program learning links a beta-cell signature to insulin secretion

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 preprint used donor-resolved non-negative matrix factorization and consensus program discovery to identify a beta-cell gene program reduced in type 2 diabetes. Learned program definitions were fixed before testing in other datasets. Higher program scores were associated with greater stimulated insulin release in donor-matched measurements from two laboratories. The laboratories shared most donors, so these are complementary measurements rather than independent biological replications, and the association does not establish a causal mechanism or treatment target.

Technology: Donor-resolved non-smooth non-negative matrix factorization; cross-donor consensus gene-program discovery

Editorial relevance 47 medicine PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
NMF learned additive gene-expression programs separately in donor/cell-type units; cross-donor consensus construction identified recurring programs before outcome testing. This learned program discovery, rather than visualization or dimensionality reduction alone, enabled the fixed beta-cell signature and its subsequent functional association tests.
Test and result
Program discovery used 48 donors. Primary independent single-cell validation used 55 eligible HPAP donors, with additional pooled Smart-seq2 and bulk analyses. Functional associations used 52 UPenn and 42 Vanderbilt donors, of whom 39 overlapped; higher frozen program scores tracked stimulated insulin release after diagnosis and preparation-quality adjustments.
Limitation / still unknown
Existing-data associations are retrospective and do not establish that the program causes secretory dysfunction. Two laboratories largely measured the same donors; bulk cell-composition adjustment was not robust across platforms. Patient-level clinical utility, causal perturbation and independent biological replication remain unestablished.

Bayesian networks learned conditional dependencies among residue interaction-energy time series in active and inactive receptor simulations. Differences in learned network participation helped select a functionally coupled pocket for virtual screening and experimental compound testing.

Finding: A November 24, 2026 preprint compared learned visual representations with macaque brain responses to static images. A Video Swin Transformer trained on fine-grained hand–object interactions matched parietal and prefrontal hand–tool geometry better than static-object and general-action models. Functional ultrasound in two other monkeys independently supported spatial alignment of tool and human-hand responses. The findings concern representational similarity; they do not establish that interaction experience caused the cortical organization or that monkeys understand tool functions.

Editorial relevance 53 biology PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Pretrained networks transformed identical images into learned feature vectors and category dissimilarity matrices. Comparing the same Video Swin architecture trained on broad actions versus fine-grained hand–object interactions enabled the specific finding that interaction-oriented learned geometry better corresponded to dorsal cortical representations.
Test and result
Whole-brain fMRI used 121 runs across three macaques; functional ultrasound sampled parietal cortex in two separate macaques. Learned category representations from four model stages or training conditions were compared with neural geometry. The interaction-trained model retained positive associations in several parietal and prefrontal regions after controlling other models and checking predictor sensitivity.

Only five animals and static stimuli were studied. Model geometries were highly correlated, with variance inflation factors of 6.40–11.71. 24 Nov 2026OpenAI

OpenAI model claims the matrix multiplication exponent is 9/4

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: OpenAI added to its openai/math repository manuscripts produced by an unnamed internal model claiming that the exponent of square matrix multiplication is exactly 9/4 over every field, with a lower bound of n^(9/4)/2 multiplication or division gates. The same release claims separations of Valiant's classes VF, VBP, VP and VNP (subsuming a VP ≠ VNP manuscript added on 23 November), circuit lower bounds for the permanent, the Erdős–Rado sunflower conjecture with an exponential bound, a strongly polynomial algorithm for linear programming (Smale's ninth problem) and a disproof of the polynomial Hirsch conjecture. The abstract describes the 9/4 upper bound as known; this record does not assess that statement against published bounds. No referee or independent review is reported.

Technology: OpenAI internal models

Editorial relevance 80 computer science Discovery sourceScientific article Suggest a correction

Publication: Announcement only · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
An unnamed internal OpenAI model produced the manuscripts; OpenAI says the VP ≠ VNP result was obtained on 6 November 2026.
Test and result
Per the repository's Lean scope notes, Lean states ω = 9/4 and the dual exponent α = 1/2 over every field in a division-free model, that allowing division preserves the square exponent, the strict inclusions VF ⊊ VBP ⊊ VP ⊊ VNP, strongly polynomial rational linear programming and a family of polytopes disproving the polynomial Hirsch conjecture. The paper's explicit lower bound of ⌈n^(9/4)/2⌉ gates at each size and several other explicit bounds remain unformalized.
Limitation / still unknown
No referee or independent expert review is reported in the reviewed material. OpenAI states that results in the collection could have issues and has withdrawn manuscripts before. Whether the Lean statements faithfully encode the standard definitions was not assessed.

24 Nov 2026Sun Yat-sen University Cancer Center; Sun Yat-sen University School of Computer Science and Engineering; Guangzhou Women and Children's Medical Center

Deep learning scores Claudin18.2 staining in gastric cancer slides across clones

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A pipeline that scores Claudin18.2 immunohistochemistry on whole-slide images used a LoRA-adapted UNI pathology foundation model for tumour recognition, adapter-based staining-intensity classification and two rounds of active learning. Agreement with pathologist-consensus 2/3+ scoring was ICC 0.849 on 195 internal test slides and 0.894 on 135 prospectively collected slides, and ICC 0.792-0.827 for three other antibody clones tested without retraining. In a non-randomised reader study, AI assistance improved every pathologist's agreement with the reference standard and shortened review time. The authors state the study is single-centre with limited prospective and cross-clone cohorts, uses expert consensus rather than absolute ground truth, may propagate errors across modules, and provides no treatment-response or outcome data; at the 50% cutoff the retrospective AUC was lower than one pathologist's.

Technology: UNI pathology foundation model (ViT-L/16) adapted with LoRA and inserted adapters, plus active learning, for tumour recognition and staining-intensity scoring.

24 Nov 2026Hunan University; Hengyang Normal University; Second Xiangya Hospital, Central South University

Generative AI designs Type I photosensitizers tested in cells and mice

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A cross-attention classifier trained on 1,338 curated photosensitizers (85.8% accuracy and ROC-AUC 0.918 on a scaffold split) and an RNN scaffold-decorating generator proposed candidates on a redesigned tetracyclic scaffold. Six were synthesised and all produced superoxide under light. The lead compound had IC50 3.3 micromolar in 4T1 cells under normoxia and 4.5 micromolar at 2% oxygen, with similar values in HepG2 and MCF-7, and markedly slowed 4T1 tumour growth in mice over 14 days with four animals per group. Validation covers one scaffold and six molecules, the lead was also phototoxic to normal HUVEC cells (IC50 3.5 micromolar under normoxia), hypoxia attenuated but did not abolish its effect, and the in vivo group size was small.

Technology: Cross-attention classifier for Type I photosensitizer activity and an RNN SMILES scaffold decorator pretrained on general chemistry data and fine-tuned on photosensitizers.

24 Nov 2026Seoul National University

Deep learning EEG metric linked to mortality in obstructive sleep apnea

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A deep-learning model trained on 22,044 polysomnography recordings estimated the apnea-hypopnea index from a single EEG channel, correlating r=0.88 with the scored index in internal testing and r=0.50 to 0.62 in three external test sets. Two derived metrics, the discrepancy between the EEG-based and scored index and an estimate from epochs without scored respiratory events, were associated with all-cause and non-cardiovascular mortality among participants free of cardiovascular disease after adjustment for the apnea-hypopnea index and clinical risk factors; the event-free estimate was also associated with cardiovascular mortality in the highest versus lowest quartile. Data came from the KISS, MESA, MrOS and SHHS cohorts. The findings are retrospective associations in a preprint, external agreement is weaker, hazard ratios and per-cohort sizes were not reviewed, and the authors suggest the discrepancy is not solely model error.

Technology: Deep-learning model estimates the apnea-hypopnea index from a single EEG channel; derived discrepancy metrics are tested against mortality.

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution

Test and result
Internal and three external test sets for index estimation, then retrospective cohort association of the derived metrics with mortality after adjustment for the apnea-hypopnea index and clinical risk factors.
Limitation / still unknown
Weaker external agreement; no causal or clinical-utility evidence; hazard ratios, covariate list, follow-up and per-cohort sizes not reviewed; not peer reviewed; one author holds related patents and leads a company.

24 Nov 2026University of Nebraska-Lincoln

Satellite machine learning measures inundation across 542,850 conserved wetland sites

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Support Vector Machine classifier applied to Sentinel-2 and Dynamic World data in Google Earth Engine mapped surface-water inundation across 542,850 wetland-related Conservation Reserve Program and Wetlands Reserve Program sites from 2018 to 2024. Measurable inundation occurred on 44.20% of CRP and 76.62% of WRP lands, persisted longer on WRP and on longer-enrolled CRP lands, and tracked hydric soil distribution. Reported validation accuracy was 95.50% on a random 75/25 split of 50,000 labelled points (RF 96.18%), but samples were split at point level so spatial autocorrelation may inflate it, there was no field hydrological validation, and surface water alone does not show conservation benefit.

Technology: Support Vector Machine (compared with Random Forest and CART) classifying land cover and water from Sentinel-2 and Dynamic World inputs.

Limitation / still unknown

Finding: A November 23, 2026 preprint reports that VirTues-M2 learned joint tissue morphology and spatial-protein representations to discover survival-associated cellular signatures in lung cancer. Signatures selected on 737 patients stratified survival in 316 held-out patients and transferred without retraining to a separate 39-patient cohort. Low-risk signatures marked organized immune-rich neighborhoods; high-risk signatures marked more disorganized tumor-rich tissue. 23 Nov 2026Balgrist University Hospital; University of Zurich

Learned MRI models link cord motion and compression level to myelopathy severity

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 23, 2026 study analyzed MRI measurements from 112 patients with degenerative cervical myelopathy. Learned hierarchical classifiers identified compression at C3/4 as a severity-associated feature. In the larger training subgroup with compression elsewhere, adding spinal cord motion improved cross-validated severity classification accuracy from 69.6% to 73.7%. Internal testing on 22 patients produced one additional correct classification. This single-center result supports a limited diagnostic association, without establishing causation, future disease progression or treatment benefit.

Technology: Decision trees, support-vector machines and kernel logistic regression; Bayesian hyperparameter optimization

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned decision trees selected compression level as an informative split; SVM and kernel-logistic classifiers tested whether dynamic cord-motion measurements add information to static MRI features for severity classification.
Test and result
A 90-patient training set and 22-patient internal test set were formed from 112 patients. In the 75-patient training subgroup without maximum compression at C3/4, adding motion improved repeated cross-validated accuracy from 69.6% to 73.7% (p=0.0059); the final internal test comparison gained one correct classification.
Limitation / still unknown
A small, single-center and imbalanced cohort with data-driven model selection remains vulnerable to overfitting. The 15-patient C3/4 training subgroup was too small for the motion-feature comparison. No external validation, causal interaction, longitudinal prognosis or treatment utility was established; acquisition and physiological confounding limit generalization.

23 Nov 2026Northwest A&F University; Adelaide University; Monash University; Beijing University of Chemical Technology

Graph-learning-guided tests identify functionally important enzyme positions

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A November 23, 2026 preprint used SCOPE, a learned structural-graph model, to prioritize enzyme positions for laboratory tests. Across four low-homology enzymes, substitutions at 11 selected positions left 0–4.1% of reference activity. The tested set included established sites and additional predicted positions, so the result is a bounded functional map rather than 11 newly discovered catalytic residues. Activity loss supports functional importance but can reflect stability or structural effects instead of direct reaction chemistry.

Technology: SCOPE DeepGCN structural-graph learning with ProtT5 sequence embeddings and a Gaussian auxiliary training objective

Editorial relevance 47 biology PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Deep graph learning combined residue-level structure, evolutionary information and protein-language-model embeddings to rank candidate functional positions. These rankings guided new functional tests, linking the learned operation to measured enzyme activity rather than only benchmark performance.
Test and result
Model-prioritized positions were tested in four enzymes using activity assays with three independent experiments and reference-enzyme normalization. Eleven tested positions had 0–4.1% residual activity; protein expression was checked. Two tested copper-coordinating sites were explicitly already established, while other model-selected positions included active-pocket and distal contexts.
Limitation / still unknown
Activity loss does not prove that every position directly catalyzes a reaction; folding, stability or subunit organization can explain some effects. Selection of the tested sites using SCOPE makes relative-model comparisons descriptive, not unbiased. Known-site recovery is not counted as a new discovery, and independent replication or broad applicability remains unestablished.

23 Nov 2026Northwestern University; Shirley Ryan AbilityLab; Karolinska Institutet; University of California, San Diego; McGill University

Cross-species classifier identifies a human dopaminergic subtype depleted in Parkinson’s disease

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A hierarchical mixture-of-experts classifier transferred mouse dopaminergic-neuron labels to human single-nucleus datasets, identifying an Anxa1-associated population. The preprint reports this population was more depleted in Parkinson’s disease than comparator subtypes in a postmortem analysis of eight control and seven Parkinson’s donors. The model uses logistic-regression experts trained on a mouse reference; donor-aware abundance models support an association rather than a causal mechanism. Cross-species label transfer, small donor cohorts and retrospective tissue limit generalization; inferred connectivity and candidate drug targets are not direct human tracing or demonstrated therapy.

23 Nov 2026University of Jyväskylä

MatterSim screening identifies low-conductivity lithium-metal candidates

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint used the MatterSim learned interatomic potential to calculate phonons for 42,347 stable, previously observed compounds and flag 11,176 with predicted phonon gaps larger than 1 THz. Screening and existing-database thermal descriptors prioritized lithium-metal binaries; targeted first-principles Boltzmann calculations then predicted room-temperature lattice thermal conductivities of 2.47, 0.63 and 0.33 W m−1 K−1 for LiPt, LiTl and LiBi, respectively. These are computational properties of known compounds, not new synthesis or experimentally measured performance. The large candidate list is model-derived; a separate DFT benchmark does not independently validate every screened material.

Technology: MatterSim v1.0.0-5M machine-learning interatomic potential

23 Nov 2026University of Texas at Austin; Flatiron Institute; Max Planck Institute of Biophysics

AlphaFold-guided tests link bacterial channel tails to temperature gating

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint combined AlphaFold2-Multimer predictions, molecular dynamics and electrophysiology to study a sodium channel from the marine bacterium Ruegeria pomeroyi. Predicted tail conformations suggested contacts that stabilize channel opening and reduce temperature sensitivity; targeted substitutions altered temperature responses in the predicted contexts. The study argues that the tail modulates gating rather than serving as a temperature sensor itself. Predictions across more than 800 bacterial sequences suggest broader conservation, but those channels were not all experimentally tested. The result concerns bacterial-channel biophysics, not a demonstrated mechanism in human neurons.

23 Nov 2026University of Strathclyde

Linear decoding distinguishes sound conditions in aging mouse cortex

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint reanalyzed auditory-cortex recordings and tested whether population activity distinguished silence, natural sounds and noise. In 33 mice with all three conditions recorded, a linear SVM achieved 82.50±8.74% mean accuracy using held-out time windows; template and linear-discriminant decoders gave similar results. Decoding remained above chance in both hearing-loss-prone and hearing-preserving strains across age groups, despite changes in individual-neuron activity. This does not establish intact perception, equivalence across ages or generalization to new mice; conditions were recorded in separate blocks.

Technology: Linear support vector machine and linear discriminant analysis

23 Nov 2026University Medical Center of the Johannes Gutenberg University Mainz; Charité Universitätsmedizin Berlin; Institute of Molecular Biology (IMB), Mainz

Machine learning links a plasma protein signature to symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In the population-based Gutenberg COVID-19 Study, mass-spectrometry plasma proteomics of 494 post-infection and 190 pre-infection samples (paired samples from 189 participants) was analyzed with bootstrap mRMR ensemble feature selection and ridge regression, identifying 49 proteins associated with symptomatic rather than asymptomatic infection (10-fold cross-validated AUC 0.85). A score from the signature was associated with acute symptom burden, disease severity and post-COVID sequelae, and higher pre-infection HDL concentrations were inversely associated with it. The authors note that feature selection was performed outside the cross-validation folds, so discrimination may be optimistic, that the signature lacks external validation, and that post-COVID symptoms were self-reported.

Technology: Bootstrap mRMR ensemble feature selection with ridge regression builds a 49-protein score from plasma proteomics.

23 Nov 2026University of California, San Francisco; U.S. Food and Drug Administration, Center for Biologics Evaluation and Research

Language model beats billing codes at reconstructing pregnancy episodes from notes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A large language model given chronologically concatenated clinical notes and messages identified pregnancy episodes and extracted gestational age, outcome, assisted-reproduction treatment and complications for 30 patients with 74 pregnancies. On a held-out set of 20 patients (54 pregnancies), checked against manual chart review, it found 53 pregnancies with 3 spurious episodes, against 40 found and 24 spurious for a hierarchical ICD-code algorithm (F1 0.96 versus 0.68), with median absolute start-date error 0 versus 25.5 days. The authors state that the two methods used different input data, so the gain reflects both the information in notes and the model; this is a single-health-system proof of concept in a preprint.

Technology: A large language model (GPT-5) extracts pregnancy episodes, dates, outcomes and complications from unstructured clinical notes.

23 Nov 2026Guizhou Center for Disease Control and Prevention; Affiliated Hospital of Guizhou Medical University

AI-measured retinal vessel metrics nominally linked to cognitive impairment in diabetes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In 630 community-dwelling adults with type 2 diabetes from four Guiyang health centres, the EVision AI system quantified retinal vascular density and fractal dimensions from fundus photographs. Mild cognitive impairment by MoCA-B affected 33.2% (n=209); after adjustment, vascular density (OR 0.987 per 1%), fractal dimension (OR 0.221 per 0.1, 95% CI 0.050-0.982) and vessel-centerline fractal dimension (OR 0.359 per 0.1) were nominally associated with lower odds, with consistent directions across age and diabetes-duration strata; sensitivity analyses supported only vascular density and centerline fractal dimension. The design is cross-sectional with MoCA-B screening rather than diagnosis, 132 participants were excluded (117 for image failure), and the associations are exploratory and not causal.

Technology: EVision AI system (deep learning and computer vision) quantifying retinal vascular density, fractal dimension and tortuosity from fundus photographs.

23 Nov 2026Addis Ababa University; Ethiopian Meteorological Institute; Alliance of Bioversity International and CIAT

Unsupervised clustering splits Ethiopian rainfall into ten seasonal regimes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Ward hierarchical and K-means clustering of 1981-2022 monthly rainfall, temperature and elevation from the Ethiopian Meteorological Institute's 4 km merged gridded data (more than 66,000 points) grouped Ethiopia into ten rainy-season regimes, with the number of clusters chosen by the elbow method, and the regimes were converted into a dry, short-rainy and main-rainy seasonal timetable. Agreement between the two clustering methods was checked monthly with the Adjusted Rand Index. Earlier station-based studies proposed five to eight zones; this preprint reports no validation against independent station records or forecasts, so the regimes are descriptive and not yet shown to improve forecasting or planning.

Technology: Unsupervised clustering (Ward hierarchical and K-means) of gridded climate variables with elbow-method cluster selection.

23 Nov 2026University of Florida; D’Or Institute for Research and Education

Deep learning on lung-screening CT estimates spirometry in two external cohorts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A three-dimensional convolutional neural network trained on 10,371 low-dose CT reconstructions from 5,975 NLST participants was fused with 20 densitometry and tracheal geometry features to predict FEV1, FVC and FEV1/FVC. Applied frozen to an external screening cohort (n = 268) and COPDGene (10,302 scans), it detected abnormal spirometry with AUROC 0.898 and 0.913, and concordance correlation coefficients were 0.80-0.88. Agreement was lower with overestimation in a COPD subgroup, the reference spirometry differed (pre- versus post-bronchodilator), and prospective utility is unproven.

Technology: 3D convolutional neural network fused with CT-derived densitometry and tracheal features, predicting FEV1, FVC and FEV1/FVC

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In a prospective diagnostic accuracy study across nine Korean stroke centres, a commercially deployed convolutional neural network classified diffusion-weighted imaging from 617 acute ischaemic stroke patients into modified TOAST categories. Agreement with expert adjudication was 75.7% (Cohen's kappa 0.643), with cardioembolism sensitivity 95.5% and small-vessel occlusion sensitivity 90.6%. Its cardioembolism score predicted newly detected atrial fibrillation with an AUC of 0.722 in 426 patients without prior AF; the authors call this exploratory and in need of confirmation.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint used trained RootPainter segmentation of root photos to estimate nodule growth in soybean plants inoculated separately with 20 rhizobial field isolates. 21 Nov 2026Max Planck Institute for Chemical Ecology; University of Georgia; Max Planck Institute for Plant Breeding Research

AlphaFold 3-guided mutagenesis identifies a candidate substrate-orienting enzyme residue

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: For an enzyme studied in a plant natural-product pathway, the authors used an AlphaFold 3 structure model with substrate docking and molecular-dynamics analysis to nominate candidate pocket residues. Mutational tests found that replacing one predicted aromatic residue abolished measured activity, while a conservative aromatic substitution restored activity, supporting a role in substrate orientation. This is a bounded residue-level mechanistic result: the authors report that the mutations do not establish a comprehensive mechanism and the predicted protein and complex structures were not experimentally characterized.

Technology: AlphaFold 3 structure prediction with substrate docking and molecular-dynamics-guided residue selection

21 Nov 2026Clemson University; University of Delaware; University of South Carolina

Sensor LSTM predicts part dimensions in an injection-molding campaign

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A shared bidirectional LSTM with four regression heads predicted weight and dimensions from injection-molding sensor sequences. The campaign collected 132 molding cycles, with 124 retained for regression. On a selected 24-cycle comparison subset, the model reported average MAPE 0.27%. This is an evaluation on the study’s campaign, with epoch selection using held-out folds. Comparators used different evaluation units and protocols, so the reported errors do not establish general model superiority. Generalization across molds, materials, machines and production periods remains untested.

Technology: Bidirectional LSTM shared encoder with task-specific MLP regression heads

Test and result
Actual molded-part weight, length, width and thickness measurements in a controlled single-factor process campaign. LSTM evaluation used shuffled-cycle five-fold splits and retained each fold’s best epoch on that fold; the cross-method comparison used selected cycles and separate setting-level DOE evaluation.
Limitation / still unknown

20 Nov 2026Anthropic

Mathematician and Claude release a Lean proof of the Riemann hypothesis

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Levent Alpöge, a mathematician at Anthropic, released a Lean library of about 17 million lines that he states proves the Riemann hypothesis and the generalized Riemann hypothesis for Dirichlet L-functions, developed with Claude. His accompanying note says he guided the argument from a toy setting while Claude supplied much of the technical work, including the √X length of a chain of theta lifts; the note's timeline runs from 6 October to 16 November 2026. A day after release, the Lean FRO and the Mathlib Initiative certified that the library proves Mathlib's statement of the Riemann hypothesis and passes several independent proof checkers. No human-readable proof has been published, and Alpöge writes that he has not yet mastered parts of the argument.

Technology: Anthropic Claude

Family Claude: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 93 mathematics Discovery source Suggest a correction

Publication: Announcement only · Test context: prospective or independent check. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Per the author, Claude carried out much of the technical development and the Lean formalization after he guided it to the setting analogous to a toy argument; he credits Claude with tens of such transfers, including the choice of chain length of theta lifts.
Test and result
The Lean FRO and Mathlib Initiative state that dozens of people reproduced compilation, that two comparison tools confirmed the proved theorem is exactly Mathlib's RiemannHypothesis statement, and that the exported proof passed Lean's kernel and several independent checkers, including formally verified ones.
Limitation / still unknown
No natural-language proof or peer-reviewed paper is available; the mathematical ideas have not yet been explained or checked by independent mathematicians. The exact Claude model version is not stated in the author's note.

20 Nov 2026University of Freiburg; Boston University; Loma Linda University

AI reconstructs maxillary denture bases from intraoral scans in a clinical feasibility study

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A PointNet++ model learned to reconstruct maxillary denture-base geometry from paired scans of 79 patients’ edentulous maxillae and existing dentures, using 57 training, seven validation and 15 test cases. Three randomly selected test outputs were manufactured and tried in patients; whole-surface registration RMSE ranged from 0.4 to 0.6 mm. Existing conventional dentures were subjectively more retentive than the newly printed AI bases. These descriptive, non-standardized try-ins do not establish clinical benefit, retention equivalence or border-specific reconstruction accuracy. Comparisons differed in material, manufacturing, prior wear and complete-denture versus base-only configuration. No objective retention or external validation was performed.

20 Nov 2026Renmin Hospital of Wuhan University; Wuhan University

Learned screening prioritizes FOS in human renal reperfusion data

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Researchers applied LASSO, random forest and support-vector-machine recursive feature elimination to renal transcriptomic data and converged on FOS and FOSB as candidates after kidney reperfusion. Both were elevated across 41 matched pre/post-reperfusion biopsy pairs, and an exploratory two-gene model reached an AUC of 0.922 on that discovery cohort; FOS alone performed similarly. In a separate dataset of 28 acute kidney injury biopsies and 11 stable controls, FOS remained elevated but FOSB did not. This is retrospective candidate evidence: the external dataset did not validate the combined diagnostic model, and the authors describe their analysis as exploratory rather than clinical model validation.

Technology: LASSO regression, random forest and support-vector-machine recursive feature elimination

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
LASSO, random forest and SVM-RFE feature selection identified FOS and FOSB from renal transcriptomic candidates; FOS was the more reproducible injury-associated candidate in the separate AKI cohort.
Test and result
Three learned feature-selection approaches converged on FOS and FOSB in GSE126805, comprising 42 kidney-transplant recipients sampled at four time points; the paper excluded one unmatched post-reperfusion sample and analyzed 41 matched pre/post pairs. The exploratory two-gene ROC AUC of 0.922 and similar FOS-alone performance were estimated in this same discovery dataset, with no held-out evaluation reported. In the independent GSE30718 series (28 AKI biopsies and 11 stable controls), FOS remained elevated but FOSB did not. The authors explicitly characterize this as candidate-feature robustness assessment, not clinical validation of a diagnostic model.
Limitation / still unknown
The combined model was not independently evaluated; FOSB did not replicate in GSE30718, and FOS alone performed comparably in the discovery cohort. The study does not establish clinical utility, prospective performance, or a causal role for FOS/FOSB in lung-to-kidney signaling.

20 Nov 2026Maharshi Dayanand University

AI-optimized magnetic nanobiocatalyst enables recyclable citrulline production

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A neural-network-assisted optimization selected a magnetic-nanoparticle formulation of arginine deiminase for citrulline production. The prepared catalyst achieved 70.0% activity recovery, near the predicted 71.18%, and 81.4% immobilization efficiency. It retained approximately 64% activity after 40 days at 4 °C, exceeded 74% after seven reuse cycles, and converted 99.1% of arginine to citrulline. These are laboratory formulation results; model training used only 30 observations and industrial or continuous-flow performance remains untested.

20 Nov 2026Manipal Academy of Higher Education

Machine learning combines plasma spectroscopy and protein profiles for exploratory endometriosis classification

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A single-center study collected plasma from 37 participants with endometriosis and 45 with other benign gynecological disorders. Seventy had paired infrared-spectroscopy and HPLC protein-profile measurements. Feature selection and a shallow neural-network classifier using mid-level data fusion achieved reported sensitivity of 80% and ROC AUC of 0.80 in repeated held-out splits with nested tuning. In a separate protein-profile analysis, K-nearest-neighbor classification and SHAP prioritized a plasma fraction for mass spectrometry, identifying five candidate proteins. This is exploratory retrospective diagnostic evidence: the cohort was small, participant ages differed between groups, there was no independent external validation, and the proteins require orthogonal validation before being considered reliable biomarkers. The work does not establish a clinically validated diagnostic test or improved patient outcomes.

20 Nov 2026University of Antwerp; University of Liège; KU Leuven; Universidad Peruana Cayetano Heredia; Yale University

Elastic-net analysis finds limited genomic separation of malaria infections in two connected Amazonian communities

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An exploratory preprint applied elastic-net logistic regression to existing genomic data from 47 Plasmodium vivax infections detected in two neighboring Peruvian Amazon communities, including 12 people reporting recent local travel. Repeated nested cross-validation yielded an AUC of 0.625 (95% bootstrap interval 0.522–0.724), versus 0.529 (0.402–0.657) after restricting the sample to non-travelers. This supports a bounded finding that fine-scale assignment was weak and sensitive to sample composition in this dataset. The analyses used different, overlapping infection sets, so their comparison does not isolate a causal effect of travel. Self-reported travel did not establish infection origin; intervals were conditional on saved predictions, and no independent cohort or operational surveillance benefit was tested.

20 Nov 2026University of Cambridge; Durham University; Stanford University

Machine-learned density functional theory reveals molecular control of electrolyte screening

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Machine-learned neural classical density functional theory trained on grand-canonical Monte Carlo data enabled calculations of asymptotic screening behavior across increasingly molecularly detailed electrolyte models. The authors report that solvent attraction creates a monotonic decay mode, solvent polarity produces re-entrant behavior, and ionic architecture changes screening-length exponents, challenging a single universal scaling law. These are computational predictions for modelled systems; the reviewed paper reports no experimental validation of the new predictions.

Technology: Neural classical density functional theory

20 Nov 2026University of Milan; IRCCS Istituto Auxologico Italiano

AlphaFold-assisted study links WFS1 dysfunction to GnRH-neuron developmental defects

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An integrated human-genetics, structural-modeling and experimental study identified WFS1 as a candidate modifier of congenital hypogonadotropic hypogonadism. AlphaFold 3 models supported simulations of rare WFS1 variants, including altered protein motions that were not captured by a simple benign/damaging prediction. Cell assays found impaired protein behavior and ER-stress activation; zebrafish knockdown disrupted GnRH-neuron architecture and reduced isl1a expression, with partial pharmacological rescue. These results support a developmental mechanism in model systems, not proof that WFS1 alone causes the human disorder or that a treatment benefits patients.

20 Nov 2026The University of Osaka; The University of Tokyo; Juntendo University

GPT-5 scoring links feedback monitoring reports with waking hippocampal ripple rates

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In a nine-participant intracranial EEG neurofeedback study, GPT-5 scored participants’ free-text reports on ten cognitive dimensions. Higher GPT-5-scored feedback monitoring was associated with lower hippocampal ripple rate (β=−2.71 events/min per SD, 95% CI −4.21 to −1.21; FDR q=.0108) across up to 27 participant-condition observations after adjustment for condition. This is an exploratory association in a small epilepsy-monitoring sample; the AI-derived ratings were not validated against human ratings or independently replicated, and do not establish causality.

20 Nov 2026University of Brawijaya; Andalas University; State University of Malang; Bandung Institute of Technology

Geo-AI mapping reveals contrasting land cover and heat patterns across four Indonesian cities

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Research Square preprint used Random Forest classification of 2025 satellite imagery to compare land cover, surface temperature and topographic wetness in four Indonesian cities. It found built-up land dominated Surakarta and Yogyakarta, while vegetation dominated Padang and Malang; Yogyakarta had the highest mean surface temperature in the analyzed scenes. 20 Nov 2026Amirkabir University of Technology

Bayesian modeling estimates configuration-dependent uncertainty in printed PLA bending

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Research Square preprint trained a Bayesian physics-informed neural network on bending data from 60 groups of printed PLA specimens and combined it with finite-element simulation. In ten within-study validation configurations, predicted uncertainty varied by raster angle and infill, with the 60-degree and reduced-infill configurations exceeding twice the estimated variation of the 0-degree configuration. All ten experimental validation values lay within the reported 95% intervals, but the sample is too small to establish precise uncertainty calibration. The result is limited to the studied printer, material and bending conditions, without separate-team replication or demonstrated service reliability.

Technology: Bayesian physics-informed neural network with Monte Carlo dropout embedded in finite-element analysis

Editorial relevance 40 materials PreprintScientific article Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A physics-constrained neural network with Monte Carlo dropout estimates flexural-modulus distributions; sampled moduli are propagated through finite-element simulations to estimate configuration-dependent prediction uncertainty.
Test and result
The PINN was developed from experimental data for 60 FDM polylactic-acid specimen groups, with ten independent configurations evaluated. Reported R² was 0.9935, MAPE 1.85%, and 95% confidence intervals contained all ten experimental validation values. Reliability analysis estimated coefficient-of-variation uncertainty of 3.8–9.0% across configurations and corresponding safety factors of 1.11–1.30.
Limitation / still unknown
The ten configurations are within-study validation, not a separate-team replication. Sample size is limited; the results do not establish transfer to other printers, materials, component geometries, or load/service conditions.
